### 3. Entry Point & CLI

**From shell:**
```bash
drain-cycle              # drain the current cycle (default)
drain-cycle --watch      # same, but open tmux pane per issue
drain-cycle --no-stack   # push to main instead of stacking
drain-cycle grade        # read run logs and print health
drain-cycle grade-draft ABA-123  # write grade markdown for one issue
drain-cycle status       # show active run progress
drain-cycle --help       # print usage
```

**From Python REPL:**
```python
from drain_cycle import cli
cli.main()  # sys.argv[1:] must be set first, or just invoke from shell
```

**Or programmatically:**
```python
import sys
from drain_cycle import orchestrator, repos, limits

# Load config
sys_repos = repos.load()
sys_limits = limits.load()

# Run the drain
sys.exit(orchestrator.run(sys_repos, sys_limits, watch=False, no_stack=False))
```

---

### 4. Core Domain Objects (Data Classes)

| Class | Fields | Responsibility |
|-------|--------|---|
| `ExecutionPlan` | `order`, `deferred` | Topo-sorted cycle issues; blocked issues |
| `Repos` | `mapping` (dict), `worktree_config_paths`, `push_to_main_repos` | Repo label → path resolver |
| `Limits` | `per_issue_tokens`, `per_issue_seconds`, `per_issue_cost_usd`, `cycle_tokens`, `cycle_seconds`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `max_resume_attempts` | Resource caps |
| `Breach` | `scope`, `metric`, `limit`, `observed` | Crossed guardrail record |
| `RunLog` | `cycle_id`, `path`, `entries`, `cycle_halt_reason` | Per-cycle execution log |
| `WorktreeHandle` | `path`, `resumed` | Prepared worktree + reuse flag |
| `WorkerResult` | `exit_code`, `breach`, `duration_seconds`, `model`, `usage`, `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `session_id`, `is_error` | Session outcome & usage |
| `HandoffData` | `pr_urls` (tuple of `PullRequest`) | Stack-mode PR submission record |
| `PullRequest` | `title`, `url` | Single submitted PR |

---

### 5. Realistic Execution Sequence

Here's what "load cycle → pick issue → spawn worker → read result" looks like in real code:

```python
# 1. Load cycle and get pending issues
from drain_cycle import linear, repos, model, prompt, worktree, worker
from pathlib import Path
import sys

# Fetch the current cycle and its pending issues
cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)

# The first executable issue (if any)
if not plan.order:
    print("No issues to drain")
    sys.exit(0)

issue = plan.order[0]
print(f"Processing: {issue['identifier']} — {issue['title']}")

# 2. Resolve repo for this issue
loaded_repos = repos.load()
target_repo = loaded_repos.resolve(issue)  # raises RepoResolutionError if no repo: label
print(f"Target repo: {target_repo}")

# 3. Prepare worktree (create or reuse)
handle = worktree.ensure(target_repo, issue['identifier'], base='main')
worktree_path = handle.path
print(f"Worktree: {worktree_path}, resumed={handle.resumed}")

# Link project config (.claude/, .mcp.json) into worktree
worktree.link_project_config(target_repo, worktree_path, loaded_repos.worktree_config_paths)

# 4. Transition Linear state
linear.set_state(issue['id'], 'In Progress')

# 5. Build prompt for the agent
agent_prompt = prompt.build(
    issue,
    worktree_path,
    resumed=handle.resumed,
    stack=True,  # stack mode
    base='main'
)
print(f"Prompt built: {len(agent_prompt)} chars")

# 6. Resolve model for this issue
worker_model = model.resolve(issue)
print(f"Model: {worker_model}")

# 7. Spawn and monitor the worker session
from drain_cycle.limits import Limits
limits_obj = Limits()

result = worker.run_issue(
    claude_cmd=['claude', '-p', '--dangerously-skip-permissions'],
    model=worker_model,
    prompt=agent_prompt,
    cwd=worktree_path,
    token_limit=limits_obj.per_issue_tokens,
    time_limit_seconds=limits_obj.per_issue_seconds,
    cost_limit_usd=limits_obj.per_issue_cost_usd,
)

print(f"Worker result: exit_code={result.exit_code}, breach={result.breach}")
print(f"  Tokens: {result.usage.get('cumulative', 0)} cumulative, "
      f"{result.usage.get('peak_context', 0)} peak")
print(f"  Cost: ${result.cost_usd or 0:.4f}")
print(f"  Turns: {result.num_turns}")

# 8. Check if Done
refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue['id'])
is_done = refreshed['state']['type'] == 'completed'
print(f"Final state: {refreshed['state']['name']}, is_done={is_done}")

# 9. Read stack-mode handoff (if submitted PRs)
from drain_cycle import handoff
if is_done:
    submitted = handoff.read(worktree_path)
    if submitted is not None:
        for pr in submitted.pr_urls:
            print(f"  Submitted: {pr.title} → {pr.url}")

# 10. Record in run log
from drain_cycle import runlog
from datetime import datetime, timezone

log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
log.append_entry(
    issue_identifier=issue['identifier'],
    started_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
    finished_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
    exit_code=result.exit_code,
    final_linear_state=refreshed['state']['name'],
    worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
    duration_seconds=result.duration_seconds,
    model=result.model,
    usage=result.usage,
    cost_usd=result.cost_usd,
    num_turns=result.num_turns,
    session_id=result.session_id,
    is_error=result.is_error,
)

print(f"Run log: {log.path}")
```

249	def _debug_enabled() -> bool:
250	    """Whether per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture is switched on.
251	
252	    Read from the environment (``os.environ`` already carries any
253	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` value loaded at CLI startup), so the operator
254	    turns it on for one investigative run with ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1

256	    """

260	def run(
261	    repos: Repos,
262	    limits: Limits | None = None,
263	    *,
264	    watch: bool = False,
265	    no_stack: bool = False,
266	) -> int:
267	    """Drain the current cycle inside the ``drain.cycle`` root span.
268	
269	    The span wrapper is thin so the body keeps its shape; per-issue work nests
270	    under it via ``_drain_one_issue``'s ``drain.issue`` spans, and the Linear,
271	    worktree, and worker spans nest under those — yielding one trace per drain.
272	    """
273	    if limits is None:
274	        limits = Limits()
275	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.cycle") as cycle_span:
276	        return _run(repos, limits, cycle_span, watch=watch, no_stack=no_stack)
277	
278	
279	def _run(
280	    repos: Repos,
281	    limits: Limits,
282	    cycle_span: Span,
283	    *,
284	    watch: bool = False,
285	    no_stack: bool = False,
286	) -> int:
287	    debug = _debug_enabled()
288	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
289	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.cycle_id", cycle_id)
290	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
291	    try:
292	        plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
293	    except DependencyCycleError as exc:
294	        halt_reason = f"Halt: {exc}"
295	        log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
296	        telemetry.mark_error(cycle_span, "err-dependency-cycle", halt_reason)
297	        console.halt(halt_reason)
298	        _emit_summary(log, total=0, halted_on=halt_reason)
299	        return 1
300	
301	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_planned", len(plan.order))
302	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.issues_deferred", len(plan.deferred))
303	
304	    if not plan.order and not plan.deferred:
305	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "nothing-to-do")
306	        console.orch(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
307	        return 0
308	
309	    console.startup_plan(
310	        cycle_id,
311	        [(i["identifier"], i["title"], model.resolve(i)) for i in plan.order],
312	    )
313	
314	    for deferred in plan.deferred:
315	        issue = deferred["issue"]
316	        blocker_id = deferred["blocker_identifier"]
317	        blocker_state = deferred["blocker_state_type"]
318	        console.orch(
319	            f"deferred {issue['identifier']} — blocked by {blocker_id} ({blocker_state})"
320	        )
321	
322	    if not plan.order:
323	        cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "all-deferred")
324	        _emit_summary(log, total=0, halted_on=None)
325	        return 0
326	
327	    in_tmux = bool(os.environ.get("TMUX"))
328	    current_pane_id: str | None = None
329	
330	    # Per-repo base-branch baton for cross-issue stacking: maps a repo name
331	    # to the branch of the last issue that submitted PRs there, so the next
332	    # same-repo issue branches its worktree off that branch (a true Graphite
333	    # stack) instead of off ``main``. Mutated in place by each drained issue.
334	    repo_baton: dict[str, str] = {}
335	
336	    total = len(plan.order)
337	    for index, issue in enumerate(plan.order):
338	        # Kill the pane from the previous issue before opening one for this issue.
339	        if current_pane_id is not None:
340	            watch_pane.close_pane(current_pane_id)
341	            current_pane_id = None
342	
343	        halt_code, current_pane_id = _drain_one_issue(
344	            issue,
345	            index=index,
346	            total=total,
347	            repos=repos,
348	            limits=limits,
349	            log=log,
350	            cycle_id=cycle_id,
351	            debug=debug,
352	            watch=watch,
353	            in_tmux=in_tmux,
354	            no_stack=no_stack,
355	            repo_baton=repo_baton,
356	        )
357	        if halt_code is not None:
358	            _emit_summary(log, total=total, halted_on=issue["identifier"])
359	            return halt_code  # type: ignore[return-value]

1	"""Minimal Linear GraphQL client.
2	
3	Reads ``LINEAR_API_KEY`` from the environment. The CLI entrypoint
4	(``cli.main``) loads ``.env`` from the drain-cycle repo root before this
5	module is exercised; a shell-exported value still takes precedence.
6	Single-team scope: hardcoded to the ``Personal`` team per README §1.
7	"""
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import os
11	from dataclasses import dataclass
12	from typing import Any
13	
14	import httpx
15	
16	from . import telemetry
17	
18	_DEFAULT_GRAPHQL_URL = "https://api.linear.app/graphql"
19	_TEAM_NAME = "Personal"
20	_PENDING_STATE_TYPES = ["backlog", "unstarted"]
21	_RESOLVED_STATE_TYPES = {"completed", "canceled"}
22	
23	
24	@dataclass(frozen=True)
25	class ExecutionPlan:
26	    """Result of ``_plan``: runnable issues in topo order + deferred issues."""
27	    order: list[dict[str, Any]]   # issues to spawn, in execution order
28	    deferred: list[dict[str, Any]]  # each {"issue", "blocker_identifier", "blocker_state_type"}
29	
30	
31	class DependencyCycleError(RuntimeError):
32	    """Raised when the blocks graph among pending issues contains a cycle."""
33	    def __init__(self, identifiers: list[str]) -> None:
34	        self.identifiers = list(identifiers)
35	        super().__init__(f"Dependency cycle among: {', '.join(identifiers)}")
36	
37	
38	def _post(
39	    query: str,
40	    variables: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
41	    *,
42	    operation: str = "graphql",
43	) -> dict[str, Any]:
44	    """Execute one GraphQL request. ``operation`` names the span and the
45	    ``linear.operation`` attribute so the five distinct calls (which all POST
46	    to the same endpoint) stay separable in Honeycomb; the httpx transport is
47	    auto-instrumented, so the raw HTTP POST appears as a child span."""
48	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span(f"linear.{operation}") as span:
49	        span.set_attribute("linear.operation", operation)
50	        key = os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_KEY")
51	        if not key:
52	            telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-linear-no-api-key", "LINEAR_API_KEY is not set")
53	            raise RuntimeError("LINEAR_API_KEY is not set")
54	        url = os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", _DEFAULT_GRAPHQL_URL)
55	        resp = httpx.post(
56	            url,
57	            headers={"Authorization": key, "Content-Type": "application/json"},
58	            json={"query": query, "variables": variables or {}},
59	            timeout=30.0,
60	        )
61	        try:
62	            resp.raise_for_status()
63	        except httpx.HTTPStatusError as exc:
64	            span.set_attribute("http.response.status_code", resp.status_code)
65	            telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-linear-http", str(exc))
66	            raise
67	        body = resp.json()
68	        if "errors" in body:
69	            telemetry.mark_error(span, "err-linear-graphql", "Linear GraphQL errors")
70	            raise RuntimeError(f"Linear GraphQL errors: {body['errors']}")
71	        return body["data"]
72	
73	
74	def current_cycle_id() -> str:
75	    """Return the active cycle id for the configured team."""
76	    data = _post(
77	        """
78	        query CurrentCycle($name: String!) {
79	          teams(filter: { name: { eq: $name } }) {
80	            nodes {
81	              id
82	              activeCycle { id }
83	            }
84	          }
85	        }
86	        """,
87	        {"name": _TEAM_NAME},
88	        operation="current_cycle",
89	    )
90	    nodes = data["teams"]["nodes"]
```

1	"""Handoff artefact a stack-mode worker leaves behind after submitting PRs.
2	
3	In stack mode the worker drives PR submission itself via the ``pr-finishing``
4	skill, which runs ``gt``/``gh`` inside the worktree, posts the Linear
5	review-summary comment, and records the submitted PRs in
6	``.drain-handoff.json`` as a ``pr_urls`` list. The orchestrator reads that
7	list back as its confirmation signal — a present, non-empty ``pr_urls`` means
8	the skill submitted at least one PR; its absence means submission never
9	completed and the per-repo chain must halt rather than march on.
10	
11	``read`` never raises: a missing or malformed file returns ``None`` so callers
12	can treat it as "not submitted yet."
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import json
17	from dataclasses import dataclass
18	from pathlib import Path
19	
20	HANDOFF_FILE = ".drain-handoff.json"
21	
22	
23	@dataclass(frozen=True)
24	class PullRequest:
25	    title: str
26	    url: str
27	
28	
29	@dataclass(frozen=True)
30	class HandoffData:
31	    pr_urls: tuple[PullRequest, ...]
32	
33	
34	def write(worktree: Path, data: HandoffData) -> None:
35	    """Serialise ``data`` to ``<worktree>/.drain-handoff.json``."""
36	    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE
37	    path.write_text(
38	        json.dumps(
39	            {
40	                "pr_urls": [
41	                    {"title": pr.title, "url": pr.url} for pr in data.pr_urls
42	                ],
43	            },
44	            indent=2,
45	        )
46	    )
47	
48	
49	def read(worktree: Path) -> HandoffData | None:
50	    """Return the handoff data for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent or invalid.
51	
52	    A valid handoff has a ``pr_urls`` list with at least one entry, each entry
53	    a dict carrying string ``title`` and ``url``. An empty list is treated as
54	    invalid: the skill writes ``pr_urls`` only after a PR is actually created,
55	    so "present but empty" means no submission happened.
56	    """
57	    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE
58	    try:
59	        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
60	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
61	        return None
62	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
63	        return None
64	    raw = payload.get("pr_urls")
65	    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not raw:
66	        return None
67	    prs: list[PullRequest] = []
68	    for entry in raw:
69	        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
70	            return None
71	        title = entry.get("title")
72	        url = entry.get("url")
73	        if not isinstance(title, str) or not isinstance(url, str) or not url:
74	            return None
75	        prs.append(PullRequest(title=title, url=url))
76	    return HandoffData(pr_urls=tuple(prs))
77

101	
102	@dataclass
103	class WorkerResult:
104	    """Outcome of one spawned session, recorded into the run-log entry.
105	
106	    ``exit_code`` is the process return code (negative when killed by a
107	    signal, e.g. ``-9`` on a guardrail SIGKILL). ``breach`` is the branch
108	    selector the orchestrator reads: ``None`` when the session ran to its
109	    own exit, otherwise the per-issue token or time cap that was crossed,
110	    carrying the cap and the value observed at kill time. ``usage`` carries
111	    the four token components plus ``cumulative`` and ``peak_context``.
112	    ``cost_usd`` / ``num_turns`` / ``session_id`` / ``is_error`` come from
113	    the terminal ``result`` event and are ``None`` when the session was
114	    killed before one arrived.
115	    """
116	
117	    exit_code: int
118	    breach: Breach | None
119	    duration_seconds: float
120	    model: str
121	    usage: dict[str, int]
122	    cost_usd: float | None
123	    num_turns: int | None
124	    session_id: str | None
125	    is_error: bool | None
126	
127	
128	def build_argv(
129	    claude_cmd: list[str],
130	    *,
131	    model: str,
132	    prompt: str,
133	    cost_limit_usd: float | None,
134	    debug_file: Path | None,
135	) -> list[str]:
136	    """Assemble the full ``claude -p`` argv for one session.
137	
138	    ``claude_cmd`` is the base argv (``["claude", "-p",
139	    "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]``); the streaming flags, ``--model``,
140	    ``--max-budget-usd`` (when ``cost_limit_usd`` is set), ``--debug-file``
141	    (when set), and the prompt are appended. The prompt is the trailing
142	    positional, so it must follow the value-taking ``--model`` /
143	    ``--max-budget-usd`` / ``--debug-file`` options, not sit between an option
144	    and its value.
145	
146	    Both spawn paths share this: the worker's own ``subprocess.Popen`` and the
147	    orchestrator's watch pane (which runs this argv under ``tee`` so the same
148	    bytes reach the operator's terminal and drain-cycle's reader).
149	    """
150	    argv = [*claude_cmd, *_STREAM_FLAGS, "--model", model]
151	    if cost_limit_usd is not None:
152	        argv += ["--max-budget-usd", f"{cost_limit_usd:g}"]
153	    if debug_file is not None:
154	        argv += ["--debug-file", str(debug_file)]
155	    argv.append(prompt)
156	    return argv
157	
158	
159	def run_issue(
160	    *,
161	    claude_cmd: list[str],
162	    model: str,
163	    prompt: str,
164	    cwd: Path,
165	    token_limit: int | None,
166	    time_limit_seconds: float | None,
167	    cost_limit_usd: float | None,
168	    passthrough: TextIO | None = None,
169	    debug_file: Path | None = None,
170	    poll_interval_seconds: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,
171	    on_progress: Callable[[int, int, int, float | None, float], None] | None = None,
172	    external_stream: TextIO | None = None,
173	    kill_fn: Callable[[], None] | None = None,
174	) -> WorkerResult:
```

405	
406	    <!-- ACT I -->
407	    <div class="act-head" id="act1">
408	      <div class="act-kicker">Structure</div>
409	      <h2 class="act-title">The split: two layers, one record</h2>
410	      <p class="act-tldr">Two layers over one authoritative record — a supervisor that drives and grades, a workflow pack that holds the procedure as skills.</p>
411	    </div>
412	
413	    <section class="s" id="s1">
414	      <header><span class="ix">§1</span><h2>Split the work into two layers, one product</h2></header>
415	      <p>The work splits into two layers:</p>
416	      <ul>
417	        <li><strong>Layer 1 — supervision (<code>drain-cycle</code>).</strong> Picks up a body of planned work and spawns a worker per phase per issue, holding the guardrails — halting, reverting, resuming, or recovering as the run demands. It then records and grades each outcome. It is vendor-agnostic: each worker is a <code>claude -p</code> (or any equivalent) subprocess.</li>
418	        <li><strong>Layer 2 — workflow (the Shaper pack).</strong> The intra-issue procedure, captured as composable skills: how a piece of work goes from picked-up to merged PR. This is the knowledge that used to live in the operator's head, written down once.</li>
419	      </ul>
420	      <p>The vision's three pieces map onto this split: "one place for state" is the state plane (§7), "the steps become skills" is Layer 2, and "the supervision becomes a supervisor" is Layer 1.</p>
421	    </section>
422	
423	    <section class="s" id="s2">
424	      <header><span class="ix">§2</span><h2>Layer 2 captures the workflow as composable skills</h2></header>
425	      <p>The pack carries two types of skills:</p>
426	      <ul>
427	        <li><strong>Planning — <code>shape:*</code>.</strong> <code>shape:idea</code>, <code>shape:project</code>, <code>shape:design</code>, <code>shape:delivery</code>.</li>
428	        <li><strong>Execution — <code>exec:*</code>.</strong></li>
429	      </ul>
430	      <div class="pipeline" aria-label="exec pipeline: exec:pickup to exec:breakdown to exec:build to (exec:debug or exec:simplify) to exec:review to exec:verify to exec:finish">
431	        <span class="pl-step">exec:pickup</span><span class="pl-arr">→</span>
432	        <span class="pl-step">exec:breakdown</span><span class="pl-arr">→</span>
433	        <span class="pl-step">exec:build</span><span class="pl-arr">→</span>
434	        <span class="pl-step pl-alt">(exec:debug | exec:simplify)</span><span class="pl-arr">→</span>
435	        <span class="pl-step">exec:review</span><span class="pl-arr">→</span>
436	        <span class="pl-step">exec:verify</span><span class="pl-arr">→</span>
437	        <span class="pl-step">exec:finish</span>
438	      </div>
439	      <p><code>exec:pickup</code> is the front door; <code>exec:review</code> fans out reviewer personas; <code>exec:finish</code> is Graphite-first with a plain-git fallback and produces the trail artefacts (What/Why/Focus PR body, review-summary comment, Linear status move).</p>
440	      <p>Every step is also a standalone skill, so a human can run any one by hand. A <strong>handoff envelope</strong> carries the issue's acceptance criteria from pickup through to review, so the spec-compliance persona can grade <em>built-the-wrong-thing</em>, not just <em>built-it-badly</em>.</p>
441	    </section>
442	
443	    <section class="s" id="s3">
444	      <header><span class="ix">§3</span><h2>Layer 1 owns process, not procedure</h2></header>
445	      <p><code>drain-cycle</code> owns process concerns only: spawn, guardrails, halt, revert, resume, recover, record, grade. It reads Linear state and the worker's artifacts to decide whether to advance to the next issue or stop. It does not contain workflow prose — the worker's prompt points at the entry skill and the procedure lives in Layer 2.</p>
446	      <p>The supervisor mechanics are documented in <code>docs/design-decisions.md</code>.</p>
447	    </section>
448	
449	    <section class="s" id="s7">
450	      <header><span class="ix">§7</span><h2>All state lives in one record — the state plane</h2></header>
451	      <p>Everything works off one organized record of where the work stands. <strong>Linear is authoritative</strong> for issue status (design decision §1, §12). Beneath it sit the durable supervisor records: per-run logs (§4, §8), the atomic active-run marker (§11), and opt-in OpenTelemetry traces (§13).</p>
452	      <p>These records are the <strong>delayed feedback loop</strong> that replaces live watching. Automating supervision deliberately gives up the operator's real-time view of each worker; observability is how that trade is paid back. Better observability widens the set of work that is safe to hand off (§9).</p>
453	    </section>
454	
455	    <!-- ACT II -->
456	    <div class="act-head" id="act2">
457	      <div class="act-kicker">Interface</div>
458	      <h2 class="act-title">How the layers meet</h2>
459	      <p class="act-tldr">The layers meet at an artifact, not a function call: Layer 2 writes signals, Layer 1 reads them. Each phase spawns on its own, so the agent that wrote the code never reviews it.</p>
460	    </div>
461	
462	    <section class="s" id="s5">
463	      <header><span class="ix">§5</span><h2>Layer 2 writes signals; Layer 1 reads them</h2></header>
464	      <p>The two layers meet at an artifact, not at a function call. <strong>Layer 2 writes signals; Layer 1 reads them.</strong> The supervisor never reads the workflow's steps — only the artifacts the workflow leaves behind: Linear issue state, run-log fields, and <code>.drain-handoff.json</code> (e.g. <code>pr_urls</code>).</p>
465	
466	      <div class="diagram diagram-boundary" role="img" aria-label="The artifact boundary: Layer 2, the workflow pack, writes down into the boundary; the boundary carries Linear issue state, run-log fields, and .drain-handoff.json (pr_urls); Layer 1, the supervisor, reads up from it.">
467	        <div class="dg-card dg-l2 dg-wide">
468	          <span class="dg-tag">Layer 2</span>
469	          <div class="dg-title">Layer 2 — the workflow pack (skills)</div>
470	          <div class="dg-sub">does the work, then leaves signals behind</div>
471	          <div class="dg-writes">WRITES ↓</div>
472	        </div>
473	        <div class="dg-boundary">
474	          <span class="dg-boundary-label">the artifact boundary</span>
475	          <div class="dg-chips">
476	            <span class="dg-chip">Linear issue state</span>
477	            <span class="dg-chip">run-log fields</span>
478	            <span class="dg-chip">.drain-handoff.json (pr_urls)</span>
479	          </div>
480	        </div>
481	        <div class="dg-card dg-l1 dg-wide">
482	          <span class="dg-tag">Layer 1</span>
483	          <div class="dg-reads">READS ↓</div>
484	          <div class="dg-title">Layer 1 — the supervisor</div>
485	          <div class="dg-sub">advances to the next issue, or halts, on whether an artifact exists</div>
486	        </div>
487	        <p class="dg-caption">Layer 1 never reads the workflow's steps — only the artifacts it leaves behind. That content-blindness is what lets the same Layer 2 run under any worker.</p>
488	      </div>
489	
490	      <p>The placement test for any new behaviour: does <code>drain-cycle</code> need to know <em>what a role does</em> (Layer 2), or only <em>whether an artifact exists</em> (Layer 1)? Submitting a PR is "what a role does" — it belongs in a skill. Reading back the submitted PR URLs to decide whether to advance is "whether an artifact exists" — it belongs in the supervisor (see design decision §19).</p>
491	      <p>Keeping the boundary at the artifact is what makes Layer 1 content-blind, and content-blindness is what lets the same Layer 2 run under any worker.</p>
492	    </section>
493	
494	    <section class="s" id="s4">
495	      <header><span class="ix">§4</span><h2>Spawn a worker per phase, not per issue</h2></header>
496	      <p>A worker does not run the whole <code>exec:*</code> chain. Each phase — code, review, finish — is its own spawn, with its own model tier, and the agent that produced an artifact never judges it. Two independent reasons force this:</p>
497	      <ul>
498	        <li><strong>Independent verification.</strong> A coder reviewing its own work rationalises its own choices. Spawning review as a separate agent that did not write the code makes review adversarial by construction, not by prompt wording.</li>
499	        <li><strong>Per-phase model economics.</strong> Review anchors to a stronger (more expensive) model regardless of what coded the change. That asymmetry is only expressible if each phase is its own spawn with its own <code>--model</code> pin — a cheap model can build while an expensive one reviews.</li>
500	      </ul>
501	      <p>The three phase agents map onto the existing pack: code = <code>exec:build</code> (+<code>exec:debug</code>, <code>exec:simplify</code>), review = <code>exec:review</code>, finish = <code>exec:finish</code>. Each is a goal-shaped worker prompt, not a resident process — the goal ("produce sliced artifacts", "apply every quality lens", "land human-readable PRs") drives a sequence of skill delegations inside one spawn.</p>
502	      <p>The cost accepted in return: every phase boundary pays a spawn plus artifact rehydration, so the handoff envelope (§5) must carry everything the next phase needs — a single worker kept that context in memory for free. This is the deliberate price of independence; see design decision §20.</p>
503	    </section>
504	
505	    <section class="s" id="s6">
506	      <header><span class="ix">§6</span><h2>Dual-mode: the same skills by hand or unattended</h2></header>
507	      <p>Because the workflow is skills and the supervisor only reads artifacts, the same skills run two ways: the operator invokes them at the keyboard, or a spawned worker runs them unattended. There is one workflow, exercised two ways — not a manual path and a separate automated path that drift apart.</p>
508	
509	      <div class="dual">
510	        <figure class="before">
511	          <figcaption>Before — I am the connector</figcaption>
512	          <pre><code>me     → "write the code for this"
513	agent  → writes it
514	me     → read it. "now review it against these standards"
515	agent  → reviews it
516	me     → "open a PR"
517	agent  → opens it
518	me     → "rewrite the PR description so it reads well"
519	agent  → rewrites it
520	me     → check it, mark it done
521	       → then start the whole chain over for the next piece of work</code></pre>
522	        </figure>
523	        <figure class="after">
524	          <figcaption>After — I built the connector</figcaption>
525	          <pre><code>me         → "work through this project"
526	supervisor → picks up the first piece of work
527	           → runs build → review → PR → finish, checking each result
528	           → marks it done, moves to the next
529	           → ...
530	           → hits one it can't get past review, stops, tells me why
531	me         → review the finished work; step in only where it halted</code></pre>
532	        </figure>
533	      </div>
534	
535	      <p>Design decision §10 (config symlinked into each worktree) is what makes the headless worker's environment match an interactive session, so a skill behaves the same in both modes.</p>
536	    </section>
537	
538	    <!-- ACT III -->
539	    <div class="act-head" id="act3">
```

## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Add a capstone REPL walkthrough to architecture.html

## Context

`docs/architecture.html` is the canonical architecture doc. It explains the two-layer
design (Layer 1 = the `drain-cycle` supervisor in Python; Layer 2 = the Shaper skill
pack) entirely in prose and div-diagrams. It never shows the actual Python.

The owner wants the doc to show **the modules a developer would touch if they opened a
REPL and invoked the project** — the high-level concepts and, above all, one place where
it all comes together end-to-end. Scope chosen: **a single capstone section**; the
existing prose sections stay untouched.

Key insight: **Layer 1 *is* the Python.** Layer 2 is skills in another repo. So the
walkthrough illustrates Layer 1, and the real orchestrator code *proves* the doc's
claims (e.g. §5's "Layer 1 reads only artifacts" is literally three function calls).

All API names below are verified against `drain_cycle/*.py` source.

## Styling precedent

`§6` already uses `<pre><code>` transcript blocks (the before/after "me → agent" pair),
styled by `main pre` / `main pre code` (architecture.html:118-120). Reuse that exact
pattern — no new CSS. REPL style: `>>>` prompts with realistic output lines, matching
"as if I ran python interactively."

## The change — one new section

Insert a new `<section class="s">` at the **end of Act II (Interface)**, immediately
before the Act III divider (architecture.html:538). It pairs naturally with §6: §6 shows
the *operator's* view (before/after prose), this shows the *supervisor's* view (the
Python loop that ties the modules together).

**Heading (achievement, not label):** e.g. "Walk one issue end to end — the supervisor's
spine in code."

**Section index:** a **non-numeric `.ix` label** (e.g. `spine` or `REPL`) rather than
`§N`, to avoid colliding with design-decision `(§N)` inline refs — exactly the ambiguity
that made the retired §8 confusing. Nav entry uses a `▸` glyph in `.navnum`.

**Lead-in (1–2 sentences):** Layer 1 is a handful of single-purpose modules; here is the
whole loop unrolled for one issue. Name the spine modules inline so the "what modules
are available" question is answered in passing:
`linear` (state plane) · `repos`/`model` (resolution) · `worktree` (isolation) ·
`prompt` (points at the entry skill) · `worker` (the metered `claude -p` spawn) ·
`handoff` + `runlog` (the artifacts read back) · `orchestrator` (the loop).

**The transcript** (one `<pre><code>`, `>>>` style, every symbol verified against
source; annotated to the section each step realizes):

```python
>>> from drain_cycle import linear, repos, model, worktree, prompt, worker, handoff, runlog
>>> from drain_cycle.limits import Limits

# 1. The state plane: what's runnable this cycle?  (§7)
>>> cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
>>> plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)        # ExecutionPlan(order=[...], deferred=[...])
>>> issue = plan.order[0]
>>> issue["identifier"], issue["title"]
('ABA-412', 'Add a retry to the handoff reader')

# 2. Isolate the work on its own branch  (design decision §10)
>>> repo   = repos.load().resolve(issue)
>>> handle = worktree.ensure(repo, issue["identifier"], base="main")
>>> linear.set_state(issue["id"], "In Progress")

# 3. Point the worker at the entry skill — Layer 1 carries no procedure  (§3)
>>> p = prompt.build(issue, handle.path, stack=True, base="main")

# 4. Spawn one metered `claude -p`, pinned to this issue's model  (§4)
>>> limits = Limits()
>>> result = worker.run_issue(
...     claude_cmd=["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"],
...     model=model.resolve(issue), prompt=p, cwd=handle.path,
...     token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
...     time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
...     cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd)
>>> result.exit_code, result.usage["cumulative"]
(0, 1843204)

# 5. Read the three artifacts back — never the worker's steps  (§5)
>>> refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
>>> refreshed["state"]["type"] == "completed"
True
>>> handoff.read(handle.path).pr_urls
(PullRequest(title='Add a retry to the handoff reader', url='https://github.com/…/pull/41'),)

# 6. Record the outcome; the loop advances to the next issue  (§7)
>>> log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
>>> log.append_entry(issue_identifier=issue["identifier"], final_linear_state="Done", …)
```

**Closer (1 sentence):** this is `orchestrator._run`'s loop unrolled for one issue —
Layer 1 in full, touching Layer 2 only through the prompt it builds and the artifacts it
reads back.

## Nav

Add the new section's `<li>` to the Act II nav group (architecture.html:252-270), with a
`▸` glyph in `.navnum` to match the non-numeric section index.

## Files

- `docs/architecture.html` — one new `<section>` + one nav `<li>`. No other files, no CSS.

## Honesty / faithfulness rules

- Every identifier and call signature is copied from verified source — no invented
  helpers. The sample issue ID, PR URL, and token count are illustrative REPL *output*
  (allowed as sample data), not API surface; render the PR URL with an ellipsis so it
  reads as a placeholder.
- The walkthrough covers only Layer 1; §2 (Layer 2 skills) stays prose-only — code
  appears where the supervisor lives, which itself reinforces the artifact boundary.

## Verification

1. Open `docs/architecture.html` in a browser (or `python -m http.server` in `docs/`):
   the code block renders in the existing `<pre>` style, legible in dark + light themes,
   and the new section appears in the left nav and scroll-spies active when scrolled to.
2. Grep each symbol in the transcript against `drain_cycle/` one final time so the doc
   can't drift from the API.
3. Re-read the section end-to-end: it reads as one continuous session and each annotation
   points at a real section.

  "new_string": "    <section class=\"s\" id=\"sspine\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">spine</span><h2>The whole supervisor is one loop</h2></header>\n      <p>§6 showed the operator's view; this is the supervisor's. Layer 1 is a handful of single-purpose modules — <code>linear</code> moves issue state, <code>worktree</code> isolates the branch, <code>prompt</code> points the worker at the entry skill, <code>worker</code> spawns the metered <code>claude -p</code>, and <code>handoff</code> and <code>runlog</code> are the artifacts read back. The run is just the loop that threads them together:</p>\n\n      <pre><code>from drain_cycle import linear, repos, model, worktree, prompt, worker, handoff, runlog\n\ndef run(repos, limits):\n    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()              # the state plane (§7)\n    plan = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)            # runnable issues, topo-sorted\n    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)\n\n    for issue in plan.order:\n        repo   = repos.resolve(issue)                 # isolate on its own branch (§10)\n        handle = worktree.ensure(repo, issue[\"identifier\"], base=\"main\")\n        linear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], \"In Progress\")\n\n        prompt_text = prompt.build(issue, handle.path, stack=True)   # entry skill, no procedure (§3)\n        result = worker.run_issue(                    # one metered `claude -p` (§4)\n            claude_cmd=[\"claude\", \"-p\", \"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"],\n            model=model.resolve(issue), prompt=prompt_text, cwd=handle.path,\n            token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,\n            time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,\n            cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,\n        )\n\n        # advance on the artifacts the worker left — never on what it did (§5)\n        done = linear.get_issue(issue[\"id\"])[\"state\"][\"type\"] == \"completed\"\n        prs  = handoff.read(handle.path)              # .drain-handoff.json → pr_urls\n        log.append_entry(issue_identifier=issue[\"identifier\"],\n                         final_linear_state=\"Done\" if done else \"In Progress\")</code></pre>\n\n      <p>That loop is <code>orchestrator.run</code>, near enough. Notice what is absent: no line reads what the worker <em>did</em>. The supervisor advances on three artifacts and nothing else — the Linear state from <code>linear.get_issue</code>, the <code>pr_urls</code> from <code>handoff.read</code>, and the entry it appends to the <code>runlog</code>. Swap <code>claude -p</code> for any other worker and the loop is unchanged.</p>\n    </section>"
}

> AGENT

Spans balance (42/42) and the four highlight rules are in place.

**Why you saw no colors:** the doc never had syntax highlighting. The `<pre>`/`<code>` rules only set a single flat text color (`--fg-soft`) and a background — so every code block, old and new, rendered monochrome. There's no highlighter library, and I didn't want to add a CDN one since the doc is deliberately self-contained (offline-readable, inline CSS/JS only).

**What I added:**
- A tiny theme-aware highlight palette (4 CSS rules) reusing the doc's existing color variables, so it adapts to dark/light automatically:
  - keywords (`from`, `import`, `def`, `for`, `in`, `if`, `else`, `True`) → `--accent` (purple), bold
  - function/method calls (`current_cycle_id`, `run_issue`, `get_issue`, `handoff.read`, …) → `--info` (blue)
  - string literals → `--ok` (green)
  - comments → `--muted` (gray), italic
- Tokenized the Python block with `<span>`s, scoped under `.code-py` so the §6 "me → agent" prose transcripts stay plain (they're not code).

Reload and the spine block should now read like a syntax-highlighted editor. Still working-tree only — say the word and I'll commit (`docs:`).

```
1	"""Per-issue execution-flow resolution.
2	
3	The ``verify`` Linear label switches a drain from the default single-worker
4	flow to the verify pipeline (Task Shaper → Implementer → Outcome Verifier →
5	PR Preparer → PR Responder). Absence of the label keeps the default flow.
6	"""
7	from __future__ import annotations
8	
9	from typing import Any
10	
11	VERIFY = "verify"
12	"""Flow name for the multi-role verify pipeline."""
13	
14	_VERIFY_LABEL = "verify"
15	
16	
17	def resolve(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:
18	    """Return ``'verify'`` if the issue carries the verify label, else ``None``."""
19	    if _VERIFY_LABEL in issue.get("labels", []):
20	        return VERIFY
21	    return None
22	
```

1	"""Stop-hook guard for spawned drain workers.
2	
3	A ``claude -p`` session that loads a "report findings / await input" skill
4	can end its turn before the completion sequence (commit + handoff + Linear
5	comment + Done) runs. The session exits cleanly with the implementation
6	green but uncommitted, and the orchestrator only sees a generic not-Done
7	halt with no signal that the work was finished.
8	
9	This module is the Stop hook the worker session runs at end-of-turn. It
10	fires only when the orchestrator has planted ``.drain-guard.json`` in the
11	worktree root, so interactive sessions and non-drain runs are a silent
12	no-op. When the marker is present and the session is trying to stop with
13	work still uncaptured (dirty tree or, in stack mode, no
14	``.drain-handoff.json``), it returns ``decision: block`` to push the
15	agent through the completion sequence. After ``max_blocks`` re-injections
16	it gives up, writes ``.drain-guard-tripped`` with the observed state, and
17	lets the session exit — the orchestrator reads that marker and tags the
18	halt as ``worker_stopped_incomplete`` rather than the generic not-Done
19	line.
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import json
24	import subprocess
25	import sys
26	from dataclasses import dataclass
27	from pathlib import Path
28	from typing import Literal, TextIO
29	
30	from .handoff import HANDOFF_FILE, read as _read_handoff
31	from .worktree import BASE_FILE
32	
33	MARKER_FILE = ".drain-guard.json"
34	TRIPPED_FILE = ".drain-guard-tripped"
35	DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCKS = 3
36	TRIPPED_HALT_REASON = "worker_stopped_incomplete"
37	
38	
39	@dataclass(frozen=True)
40	class GuardConfig:
41	    """State written by the orchestrator and updated by the hook.
42	
43	    ``mode`` is ``"stack"`` (handoff-driven assembly) or ``"push"`` (direct
44	    push to main). ``count`` is the number of times this hook has already
45	    returned ``block`` for the session — incremented in place. ``max_blocks``
46	    is the cap before the guard gives up.
47	    """
48	
49	    mode: str
50	    count: int
51	    max_blocks: int
52	
53	
54	def write_marker(
55	    worktree: Path,
56	    *,
57	    mode: Literal["stack", "push"],
58	    max_blocks: int = DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCKS,
59	) -> None:
60	    """Plant the per-session marker the hook keys on.
61	
62	    Called by the orchestrator immediately before spawning a worker. Replaces
63	    any prior marker so a resumed worktree starts fresh from ``count=0``.
64	    Also clears any stale tripped-marker from a previous attempt so the
65	    orchestrator's post-session read isn't a false positive.
66	    """
67	    (worktree / MARKER_FILE).write_text(
68	        json.dumps({"mode": mode, "count": 0, "max_blocks": max_blocks})
69	    )
70	    tripped = worktree / TRIPPED_FILE
71	    if tripped.exists():
72	        tripped.unlink()
73	
74	
75	def read_marker(worktree: Path) -> GuardConfig | None:
76	    """Return the guard state for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent/invalid."""
77	    path = worktree / MARKER_FILE
78	    try:
79	        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
80	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
81	        return None
82	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
83	        return None
84	    mode = payload.get("mode")
85	    count = payload.get("count")
86	    max_blocks = payload.get("max_blocks")
87	    if mode not in ("stack", "push"):
88	        return None
89	    if not isinstance(count, int) or not isinstance(max_blocks, int):
90	        return None
91	    return GuardConfig(mode=mode, count=count, max_blocks=max_blocks)
92	
93	
94	def _write_marker_count(worktree: Path, config: GuardConfig, *, count: int) -> None:
95	    (worktree / MARKER_FILE).write_text(
96	        json.dumps({"mode": config.mode, "count": count, "max_blocks": config.max_blocks})
97	    )
98	
99	
100	def read_tripped(worktree: Path) -> str | None:
101	    """Return the tripped reason for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if not tripped."""
102	    path = worktree / TRIPPED_FILE
103	    try:
104	        return path.read_text().strip() or None
105	    except OSError:
106	        return None
107	
108	
109	# drain-cycle's own per-session artefacts live at the worktree root and are
110	# expected to be untracked. ``_git_dirty`` filters them out so the guard
111	# doesn't see itself (or the handoff file written *after* the commit) as
112	# uncaptured work.
113	_OWN_ARTEFACTS = frozenset({MARKER_FILE, TRIPPED_FILE, HANDOFF_FILE, BASE_FILE})
114	
115	
116	def _git_dirty(worktree: Path) -> bool:
117	    """Return True if the worktree has uncommitted changes (excluding our own artefacts).
118	
119	    ``git status --porcelain`` lines are ``XY <path>`` (two status chars,
120	    a space, then the path). Untracked entries for our own artefacts —
121	    ``.drain-guard.json``, ``.drain-guard-tripped``, ``.drain-handoff.json``
122	    — are skipped: the handoff is written after the commit by design, and
123	    the guard's own markers are not the agent's work.
124	    """
125	    result = subprocess.run(
126	        ["git", "status", "--porcelain"],
127	        cwd=str(worktree),
128	        check=False,
129	        capture_output=True,
130	        text=True,
131	    )
132	    if result.returncode != 0:
133	        return False
134	    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():
135	        if len(line) < 4:
136	            continue
137	        path = line[3:].strip()
138	        if path in _OWN_ARTEFACTS:
139	            continue
140	        return True
141	    return False
142	
143	
144	def _has_handoff(worktree: Path) -> bool:
145	    return _read_handoff(worktree) is not None
146	
147	
148	@dataclass(frozen=True)
149	class Decision:
150	    """The hook's response to one Stop event.
151	
152	    ``block_reason`` is non-None when the agent should be re-prompted (the
153	    hook emits ``{"decision": "block", "reason": ...}``). ``tripped_reason``
154	    is non-None when the guard has given up and written ``TRIPPED_FILE`` —
155	    the hook then exits silently and the orchestrator surfaces the tripped
156	    state in the halt line.
157	    """
158	
159	    block_reason: str | None
160	    tripped_reason: str | None
161	
162	
163	_BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK = (
164	    "drain-cycle stop-guard: the issue is not finished — the worktree has "
165	    "uncommitted changes or no submitted PRs in .drain-handoff.json yet. "
166	    "Complete the remaining steps now: commit any pending changes to the "
167	    "issue branch as reviewable slices (do not push by hand), run "
168	    "`/shape:pr-finishing` to submit the stacked PR(s) — it writes the "
169	    "pr_urls into .drain-handoff.json and posts the review-summary comment "
170	    "— then transition the issue to Done. If you are genuinely blocked, "
171	    "leave the issue In Progress and post a comment naming the blocker — "
172	    "do not stop silently."
173	)
174	
175	_BLOCK_PROMPT_PUSH = (
176	    "drain-cycle stop-guard: the issue is not finished — the worktree has "
177	    "uncommitted changes. Complete the remaining steps now: commit and push "
178	    "to main, post a review-summary comment on the Linear issue, then "
179	    "transition the issue to Done. If you are genuinely blocked, leave the "
180	    "issue In Progress and post a comment naming the blocker — do not stop "
181	    "silently."
182	)
183	
184	
185	def evaluate(worktree: Path, *, stop_hook_active: bool) -> Decision:
186	    """Decide whether to block the Stop event, give up, or pass through.
187	
188	    The decision tree, in order:
189	
190	    * No marker → not a drain worker session. Pass through.
191	    * Stack mode + clean tree + valid handoff → completion sequence ran.
192	      Pass through.
193	    * Push mode + clean tree → completion sequence ran. Pass through.
194	    * Otherwise: incomplete. If ``count < max_blocks`` (and we're not
195	      already deep in a block-loop per Claude's ``stop_hook_active`` flag),
196	      increment and emit a block. If at the cap, write the tripped file
197	      and pass through so the session can exit cleanly.
198	
199	    ``stop_hook_active`` mirrors Claude's payload — when True the hook is
200	    being re-invoked from within a previous block, so the cap applies even
201	    if the count somehow lagged.
202	    """
203	    config = read_marker(worktree)
204	    if config is None:
205	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=None)
206	
207	    dirty = _git_dirty(worktree)
208	    handoff_ok = _has_handoff(worktree) if config.mode == "stack" else True
209	    complete = (not dirty) and handoff_ok
210	    if complete:
211	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=None)
212	
213	    state_summary = _describe_incomplete(config.mode, dirty=dirty, handoff_ok=handoff_ok)
214	
215	    if config.count >= config.max_blocks or (stop_hook_active and config.count > 0):
216	        reason = f"{state_summary} after {config.count} re-injection(s)"
217	        (worktree / TRIPPED_FILE).write_text(reason)
218	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=reason)
219	
220	    _write_marker_count(worktree, config, count=config.count + 1)
221	    prompt = _BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK if config.mode == "stack" else _BLOCK_PROMPT_PUSH
222	    return Decision(block_reason=prompt, tripped_reason=None)
223	
224	
225	def _describe_incomplete(mode: str, *, dirty: bool, handoff_ok: bool) -> str:
226	    parts = []
227	    if dirty:
228	        parts.append("uncommitted changes in worktree")
229	    if mode == "stack" and not handoff_ok:
230	        parts.append("no valid .drain-handoff.json")
231	    return ", ".join(parts) or "incomplete state"
232	
233	
234	def run(stdin: TextIO, stdout: TextIO, *, cwd: Path | None = None) -> int:
235	    """Read the Stop hook payload from ``stdin`` and emit a decision on ``stdout``.
236	
237	    Exit code 0 always; the decision is carried in the JSON body. Errors
238	    parsing the payload fall through to a silent pass — a broken hook must
239	    not block a legitimate stop.
240	    """
241	    try:
242	        payload = json.load(stdin)
243	    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):
244	        payload = {}
245	    stop_hook_active = bool(payload.get("stop_hook_active"))
246	    worktree = cwd if cwd is not None else Path(payload.get("cwd") or ".").resolve()
247	
248	    decision = evaluate(worktree, stop_hook_active=stop_hook_active)
249	    if decision.block_reason is not None:
250	        json.dump({"decision": "block", "reason": decision.block_reason}, stdout)
251	    return 0
252	
253	
254	def main() -> None:
255	    sys.exit(run(sys.stdin, sys.stdout))
256	
```

1	"""Self-grade a series of drain-cycle runs.
2	
3	Reads every ``~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json`` produced by the orchestrator
4	and emits a human-readable health read:
5	
6	1. Per-cycle section: cycle_id, attempted count, integer completion %,
7	   and halted entries as
8	   ``<identifier>: (<final_linear_state>, <exit_code>)``.
9	2. Across-cycles section: trend label over the last ``_TREND_WINDOW``
10	   cycles + recurrent ``(final_linear_state, exit_code)`` tuples
11	   appearing in ≥2 of those cycles.
12	3. Verdict section: one of ``HEALTHY`` / ``WATCH`` / ``CONCERNING``
13	   against the most-recent cycle's completion %. Labels are general
14	   health bands — the operator interprets them against whatever kill /
15	   continuation rule they hold for the work being drained. No
16	   project-specific reminders are baked into the tool.
17	
18	Run logs are grouped by ``cycle_id`` because one cycle can produce
19	multiple files when ``drain-cycle`` is re-run against the same cycle
20	after a halt. Chronological ordering uses the earliest filename per
21	cycle.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	import json
26	import sys
27	from collections import Counter
28	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
29	from pathlib import Path
30	from typing import Any
31	
32	from . import runlog
33	
34	_TREND_WINDOW = 3
35	"""Number of most-recent cycles considered by the trend + recurrent-tuple
36	analysis. Pinned here; not configurable by the CLI."""
37	
38	_HEALTHY_THRESHOLD = 80
39	_WATCH_THRESHOLD = 50
40	"""Verdict bands. Boundaries are inclusive on the lower edge:
41	≥80 → HEALTHY, 50–79 → WATCH, <50 → CONCERNING."""
42	
43	
44	def default_runs_dir() -> Path:
45	    return runlog.runs_dir()
46	
47	
48	@dataclass
49	class _Cycle:
50	    cycle_id: str
51	    entries: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
52	    earliest_filename: str = ""
53	
54	    @property
55	    def attempted(self) -> int:
56	        return len(self.entries)
57	
58	    @property
59	    def completion_percent(self) -> int:
60	        if not self.entries:
61	            return 0
62	        done = sum(1 for e in self.entries if e["final_linear_state"] == "Done")
63	        return round(done * 100 / self.attempted)
64	
65	    @property
66	    def halted(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
67	        return [e for e in self.entries if e["final_linear_state"] != "Done"]
68	
69	
70	def _collect_cycles(runs_dir: Path) -> list[_Cycle]:
71	    """Group run-log files by cycle_id, chronological by earliest filename.
72	
73	    A cycle can span multiple files (re-running drain-cycle on the same
74	    cycle writes a new file). Ties are broken by filename; in practice
75	    cycles never tie because each cycle has its own UUID, but the rule is
76	    held for determinism if two cycles ever did share a min-filename
77	    timestamp.
78	
79	    Malformed files — invalid JSON, missing ``cycle_id`` — are skipped
80	    with a stderr warning. The grade tool is the kill-condition gauge;
81	    one bad file should never block reading the rest.
82	    """
83	    files = sorted(runs_dir.glob("*.json"))
84	    cycles: dict[str, _Cycle] = {}
85	    for path in files:
86	        try:
87	            payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
88	            cycle_id = payload["cycle_id"]
89	        except (json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, OSError) as exc:
90	            print(
91	                f"drain-cycle grade: skipping malformed run-log {path}: {exc}",
92	                file=sys.stderr,
93	            )
94	            continue
95	        cycle = cycles.setdefault(cycle_id, _Cycle(cycle_id=cycle_id))
96	        cycle.entries.extend(payload.get("entries", []))
97	        if not cycle.earliest_filename or path.name < cycle.earliest_filename:
98	            cycle.earliest_filename = path.name
99	    return sorted(cycles.values(), key=lambda c: (c.earliest_filename, c.cycle_id))
100	
101	
102	def _trend_label(percents: list[int]) -> str:
103	    """Strict-monotonic over the window → improving/regressing; else flat.
104	
105	    Single-cycle or empty windows are "flat" by definition (no direction
106	    can be inferred from one or zero points). Two-cycle windows still
107	    apply the strict-monotonic rule (50 → 60 is improving; 50 → 50 is
108	    flat because the inequality is strict).
109	    """
110	    if len(percents) < 2:
111	        return "flat"
112	    if all(a < b for a, b in zip(percents, percents[1:])):
113	        return "improving"
114	    if all(a > b for a, b in zip(percents, percents[1:])):
115	        return "regressing"
116	    return "flat"
117	
118	
119	def _recurrent_tuples(cycles: list[_Cycle]) -> list[tuple[tuple[str, int], int]]:
120	    """Tuples appearing in ≥2 of the supplied cycles, with cycle-count.
121	
122	    Counted per cycle, not per entry: if a tuple appears three times
123	    inside one cycle and once in another, it counts as 2 cycles (not 4).
124	    Sorted by descending count then by the tuple itself for determinism.
125	    """
126	    counter: Counter[tuple[str, int]] = Counter()
127	    for cycle in cycles:
128	        seen_in_cycle: set[tuple[str, int]] = set()
129	        for entry in cycle.entries:
130	            key = (entry["final_linear_state"], entry["exit_code"])
131	            seen_in_cycle.add(key)
132	        counter.update(seen_in_cycle)
133	    recurrent = [(tup, count) for tup, count in counter.items() if count >= 2]
134	    recurrent.sort(key=lambda item: (-item[1], item[0]))
135	    return recurrent
136	
137	
138	def _render_across_cycles(cycles: list[_Cycle]) -> str:
139	    window = cycles[-_TREND_WINDOW:]
140	    percents = [c.completion_percent for c in window]
141	    lines = [
142	        f"window: last {len(window)} of {len(cycles)} cycle(s)"
143	        f" (max {_TREND_WINDOW})",
144	        f"trend: {_trend_label(percents)}",
145	    ]
146	    recurrent = _recurrent_tuples(window)
147	    if not recurrent:
148	        lines.append("recurrent tuples: none")
149	    else:
150	        lines.append("recurrent tuples:")
151	        for (state, exit_code), count in recurrent:
152	            lines.append(f"  ({state}, {exit_code}) x {count}")
153	    return "\n".join(lines)
154	
155	
156	def _render_verdict(cycles: list[_Cycle]) -> str:
157	    most_recent = cycles[-1]
158	    pct = most_recent.completion_percent
159	    if pct >= _HEALTHY_THRESHOLD:
160	        label = "HEALTHY"
161	    elif pct >= _WATCH_THRESHOLD:
162	        label = "WATCH"
163	    else:
164	        label = "CONCERNING"
165	    return f"verdict: {label} (most-recent cycle completion: {pct}%)"
166	
167	
168	def _render_per_cycle(cycle: _Cycle) -> str:
169	    lines = [
170	        f"cycle_id: {cycle.cycle_id}",
171	        f"  attempted: {cycle.attempted}",
172	        f"  completion: {cycle.completion_percent}%",
173	    ]
174	    if cycle.halted:
175	        lines.append("  halted:")
176	        for entry in cycle.halted:
177	            lines.append(
178	                f"    {entry['issue_identifier']}: "
179	                f"({entry['final_linear_state']}, {entry['exit_code']})"
180	            )
181	    return "\n".join(lines)
182	
183	
184	def run(runs_dir: Path) -> int:
185	    cycles = _collect_cycles(runs_dir) if runs_dir.is_dir() else []
186	    if not cycles:
187	        print(
188	            f"drain-cycle grade: no run logs found at {runs_dir}",
189	            file=sys.stderr,
190	        )
191	        return 1
192	
193	    print("== Per-cycle ==")
194	    print()
195	    for cycle in cycles:
196	        print(_render_per_cycle(cycle))
197	        print()
198	
199	    print("== Across cycles ==")
200	    print()
201	    print(_render_across_cycles(cycles))
202	    print()
203	
204	    print("== Verdict ==")
205	    print()
206	    print(_render_verdict(cycles))
207	    print()
208	    return 0
209	
```

> AGENT

Read all three. Here's my assessment — ranked by how much each carries a *concept* the doc is currently asserting but not showing, with an eye to your review-workflow addition.

## Worth adding

**1. `stop_guard.py` — the integrity guarantee under the artifact boundary. (Strongest.)**

§5 claims Layer 1 "advances on whether an artifact exists." That only holds if a worker *can't* mark Done while leaving the artifacts unwritten. `stop_guard` is what makes the claim true: it's a Stop hook planted in the worktree (`.drain-guard.json`) that fires at the worker's end-of-turn, and if the tree is dirty or (in stack mode) there's no `.drain-handoff.json`, it returns `decision: block` to push the agent back through the completion sequence — up to `max_blocks` times, then writes `.drain-guard-tripped` and lets the orchestrator tag the halt `worker_stopped_incomplete` instead of a generic not-Done.

Why it's the one to add: it's **the structural twin of your future review-workflow.** Its loop — *watch the worker's output → block → re-inject a corrective prompt → retry → give up and tag* — is exactly the shape of *PR comment → revise → resubmit → record in `responder_runs[]` → escalate on a bad fix*. Documenting `stop_guard` now establishes the pattern ("Layer 1 closes a loop on the worker's output via a hook"), so the review-workflow section reads as a second instance of a known idea rather than a one-off. They'd sit well adjacent — both are §5-adjacent "enforcement" mechanics.

**2. `grade.py` — the trust gauge that closes the autonomy-horizon loop. (Medium-strong.)**

The lead promises Layer 1 "records and grades each outcome," and §9 is built on "observability earns trust" — but nothing in the body shows the instrument. `grade.py` is it: reads every run-log, computes per-cycle completion %, a `improving / flat / regressing` trend over the last 3 cycles, recurrent `(final_linear_state, exit_code)` failure tuples, and a `HEALTHY / WATCH / CONCERNING` verdict. That's the literal feedback loop §9 describes — *drain → record → grade → decide whether to widen the horizon.* A short snippet or callout in §9 would ground an otherwise abstract section. (Honest caveat to keep: the verdict bands are generic; the operator supplies the kill rule.)

## The seam for your review-workflow (don't document as built yet)

**`flow.py` + the verify pipeline + `responder_runs[]`.** `flow.resolve` already routes a `verify`-labelled issue to a named pipeline: *Task Shaper → Implementer → Outcome Verifier → PR Preparer → **PR Responder***. The slot for your review-workflow already exists in the code, and the run-log already carries `responder_runs[]` (shipped empty). But the verdict/responder roles are placeholders — the orchestrator sets `outcome_verdict = None  # populated by M2+ roles` and `responder_runs = []`. So I'd **not** put this in the "as-is" doc now; just know that's where your section plugs in, and design-decision §23 already frames the PR Responder as **control-plane behaviour, not a pack skill** — that's the architectural decision your future section should open from.

## Not worth a section

`telemetry`/`progress` (already nodded to in §7 as the delayed feedback loop), `limits`/`repos`/`model` (plumbing, already shown in the spine), and `watch`/`watch_pane` — the tmux live-pane is an operator convenience that actually cuts *against* the doc's "give up live watching, pay it back with observability" narrative, so highlighting it would muddy §7.

---

My recommendation: add **`stop_guard`** (as an enforcement counterpart near §5) and a **`grade`** snippet in §9 now — both are built, both close loops the doc already asserts — and leave a clean architectural slot that your review-workflow section fills later, mirroring `stop_guard`'s shape. Want me to draft either one?

561	        result = worker.<span class="tok-fn">run_issue</span>(                    <span class="tok-com"># one metered `claude -p` (§4)</span>
562	            claude_cmd=[<span class="tok-str">"claude"</span>, <span class="tok-str">"-p"</span>, <span class="tok-str">"--dangerously-skip-permissions"</span>],
563	            model=model.<span class="tok-fn">resolve</span>(issue), prompt=prompt_text, cwd=handle.path,
564	            token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
565	            time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
566	            cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
567	        )
568	
569	        <span class="tok-com"># advance on the artifacts the worker left — never on what it did (§5)</span>
570	        done = linear.<span class="tok-fn">get_issue</span>(issue[<span class="tok-str">"id"</span>])[<span class="tok-str">"state"</span>][<span class="tok-str">"type"</span>] == <span class="tok-str">"completed"</span>
571	        prs  = handoff.<span class="tok-fn">read</span>(handle.path)              <span class="tok-com"># .drain-handoff.json → pr_urls</span>
572	        log.<span class="tok-fn">append_entry</span>(issue_identifier=issue[<span class="tok-str">"identifier"</span>],
573	                         final_linear_state=<span class="tok-str">"Done"</span> <span class="tok-kw">if</span> done <span class="tok-kw">else</span> <span class="tok-str">"In Progress"</span>)</code></pre>
574	
575	      <p>That loop is <code>orchestrator.run</code>, near enough. Notice what is absent: no line reads what the worker <em>did</em>. The supervisor advances on three artifacts and nothing else — the Linear state from <code>linear.get_issue</code>, the <code>pr_urls</code> from <code>handoff.read</code>, and the entry it appends to the <code>runlog</code>. Swap <code>claude -p</code> for any other worker and the loop is unchanged.</p>
576	    </section>
577	
578	    <!-- ACT III -->
579	    <div class="act-head" id="act3">
580	      <div class="act-kicker">Scale</div>
581	      <h2 class="act-title">How far it runs</h2>
582	      <p class="act-tldr">The supervisor reaches further as observability earns trust: from one-shot CLI to resident daemon, from one issue to a whole planned unit, with review rolling up the same tree the work was broken down along.</p>
583	    </div>
584	
585	    <section class="s" id="s9">
586	      <header><span class="ix">§9</span><h2>Extend the autonomy horizon as observability earns trust</h2></header>
587	      <p>How far the supervisor runs without the operator. Today it drains a cycle transactionally — one issue, one worker, one diff — and halts when it runs out of runnable work or hits a result it cannot get past. The horizon extends outward as observability earns more trust: building on unmerged work, auto-merging trusted classes, and eventually a resident process that watches its own PRs through the review-and-merge loop. That extension is shaped in <a href="ideas/drain-past-the-merge-gate.md"><code>ideas/drain-past-the-merge-gate.md</code></a>.</p>
588	      <p>The horizon is a deliberate trade, not a default: autonomy is applied selectively where the delayed feedback loop (§7) makes the loss of live intervention acceptable.</p>
589	    </section>
```

  "new_string": "      <p>Keeping the boundary at the artifact is what makes Layer 1 content-blind, and content-blindness is what lets the same Layer 2 run under any worker.</p>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"sguard\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">guard</span><h2>Done has to mean done — the stop-guard enforces it</h2></header>\n      <p>Content-blindness has one precondition: a worker must not be able to <em>stop</em> with the work uncaptured. A <code>claude -p</code> session that loads a \"report findings and await input\" skill can end its turn with the code green but uncommitted and no PR submitted — the boundary would then read \"not Done\" for work that was actually finished, or worse, let the run march on past a half-done issue. The <strong>stop-guard</strong> closes that gap. Before each spawn the orchestrator plants <code>.drain-guard.json</code> in the worktree; the worker runs <code>stop_guard</code> as its Stop hook, so at end-of-turn Layer 1 gets one last check on the artifacts before the session is allowed to exit:</p>\n\n      <pre class=\"code-py\"><code><span class=\"tok-kw\">def</span> <span class=\"tok-fn\">evaluate</span>(worktree, *, stop_hook_active):\n    config = <span class=\"tok-fn\">read_marker</span>(worktree)             <span class=\"tok-com\"># planted before the spawn; absent ⇒ not a drain worker</span>\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">if</span> config <span class=\"tok-kw\">is</span> <span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>:\n        <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-fn\">Decision</span>(block_reason=<span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>, tripped_reason=<span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>)   <span class=\"tok-com\"># silent no-op for interactive sessions</span>\n\n    dirty      = <span class=\"tok-fn\">_git_dirty</span>(worktree)          <span class=\"tok-com\"># uncommitted work in the tree?</span>\n    handoff_ok = <span class=\"tok-fn\">_has_handoff</span>(worktree) <span class=\"tok-kw\">if</span> config.mode == <span class=\"tok-str\">\"stack\"</span> <span class=\"tok-kw\">else</span> <span class=\"tok-kw\">True</span>\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">if</span> <span class=\"tok-kw\">not</span> dirty <span class=\"tok-kw\">and</span> handoff_ok:                <span class=\"tok-com\"># commit + handoff present ⇒ the sequence ran</span>\n        <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-fn\">Decision</span>(block_reason=<span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>, tripped_reason=<span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>)\n\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">if</span> config.count &gt;= config.max_blocks:    <span class=\"tok-com\"># out of retries ⇒ give up and tag it</span>\n        (worktree / TRIPPED_FILE).<span class=\"tok-fn\">write_text</span>(reason)   <span class=\"tok-com\"># orchestrator reads → worker_stopped_incomplete</span>\n        <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-fn\">Decision</span>(block_reason=<span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>, tripped_reason=reason)\n\n    <span class=\"tok-fn\">_write_marker_count</span>(worktree, config, count=config.count + 1)\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-fn\">Decision</span>(block_reason=_BLOCK_PROMPT, tripped_reason=<span class=\"tok-kw\">None</span>)   <span class=\"tok-com\"># re-inject: finish the sequence, then stop</span></code></pre>\n\n      <p>The shape generalises: Layer 1 watches what a worker leaves behind, and when it falls short, re-injects a corrective prompt and retries before giving up and recording the shortfall. The same loop — observe, push back, retry, then escalate — is how the supervisor will later answer review comments on an open PR: revise, resubmit, and record the pass, escalating to the operator when a fix keeps missing.</p>\n    </section>\n"
}

  "new_string": "      <p>The horizon is a deliberate trade, not a default: autonomy is applied selectively where the delayed feedback loop (§7) makes the loss of live intervention acceptable.</p>\n      <p>What earns that trust is measured, not felt. <code>grade</code> reads every run-log under <code>~/.drain-cycle/runs/</code> and scores the series — a Done-rate per cycle, a trend across the last three, the <code>(final_linear_state, exit_code)</code> failures that recur, and a verdict band the operator reads against their own kill rule:</p>\n\n      <pre class=\"code-py\"><code><span class=\"tok-kw\">def</span> <span class=\"tok-fn\">verdict</span>(cycles):                              <span class=\"tok-com\"># cycles built from ~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json</span>\n    recent = [c.completion_percent <span class=\"tok-kw\">for</span> c <span class=\"tok-kw\">in</span> cycles[-3:]]   <span class=\"tok-com\"># Done-rate per cycle</span>\n    trend  = <span class=\"tok-fn\">_trend_label</span>(recent)                 <span class=\"tok-com\"># improving / flat / regressing</span>\n    pct    = recent[-1]\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">if</span> pct &gt;= 80:  <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-str\">\"HEALTHY\"</span>               <span class=\"tok-com\"># the horizon may widen</span>\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">if</span> pct &gt;= 50:  <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-str\">\"WATCH\"</span>\n    <span class=\"tok-kw\">return</span> <span class=\"tok-str\">\"CONCERNING\"</span>                          <span class=\"tok-com\"># pull it back</span></code></pre>\n\n      <p>Records (§7) feed grades; grades decide whether the horizon widens. That closed loop — drain, record, grade, then choose how far to reach next — is the delayed feedback loop paying back the live view the operator gave up.</p>\n    </section>"
}
```

```
1	# Style Rules (all document types)
2	
3	Apply these during Phase 4. The goal is the shortest path to the reader's mind without losing operational facts.
4	
5	## Sentence mechanics
6	
7	**Characters as subjects, actions as verbs.** Find the doer; make it the subject; make its activity a specific verb.
8	
9	| Bad | Good |
10	|---|---|
11	| "There was a failure in the deployment process due to configuration drift." | "The deploy failed because the staging config drifted from prod." |
12	| "Request dropping occurs at high utilization." | "The load balancer drops requests above 1k RPS." |
13	| "It was decided that caching should be utilized." | "We will cache session tokens in Redis." |
14	
15	**Old before new.** Start with what the reader knows; end with the new term. The end of a sentence is the stress position — the reader emphasizes whatever sits there.
16	
17	| Bad | Good |
18	|---|---|
19	| "Edge Workers, which intercept requests at the CDN layer, will reduce load time." | "To cut load time, we will intercept requests at the CDN layer using Edge Workers." |
20	
21	**Hand-product test.** Every task, goal, or milestone must imply a tangible end product the reader can visualize. "Investigate latency" fails; "a report naming the three slowest queries in /orders" passes.
22	
23	## Nominalizations — reverse them
24	
25	Abstract nouns derived from verbs freeze action. Turn them back into verbs.
26	
27	| Frozen | Active |
28	|---|---|
29	| utilization | use |
30	| implementation | implement |
31	| optimization | optimize |
32	| investigation | investigate |
33	| facilitation | help / enable |
34	| determination | decide |
35	| reduction | reduce / cut |
36	
37	## Vocabulary watchlist
38	
39	Sweep the document for these and replace:
40	
41	| Avoid | Prefer | Why |
42	|---|---|---|
43	| load-bearing / vital | essential / required | Overworked metaphor distracts from the technical reality |
44	| bottleneck | shortage / delay / hold-up | Cliché that hides the specific cause of constriction |
45	| target | objective / goal / aim | Often "attained" without being "hit" |
46	| blueprint | plan / scheme / spec | Implies a final design; usually misused for early ideas |
47	| ceiling | limit / maximum | "Raise the limit" is clearer than "raise the ceiling" |
48	| interface with | talk to | Jargon for a plain act |
49	| impact (as verb) | affect | "Impact" blunts a painful truth |
50	| leverage (as verb) | use | Same |
51	| basically / actually / quite / virtually / a bit | *(delete)* | Little qualifiers dilute authority |
52	| "at this point in time" | now | Shortest path to the mind |
53	| "in order to" | to | Same |
54	| "It should be noted that…" / "It is important to…" | *(start directly)* | Throat-clearing adds nothing |
55	| full and complete / each and every | full / each | Redundant pairs |
56	
57	## Keep the exact term; cut only the hiding metaphor
58	
59	Use the precise technical term whenever it is accurate — `mutex`, `P99`, `idempotent` earn their place. Replace a metaphor only when it hides the cause the reader needs. A figure of speech that names no mechanism leaves the reader guessing; the concrete noun states it.
60	
61	| Hides the cause | States it |
62	|---|---|
63	| "The seam lives in the orchestrator." | "The recovery points are in the orchestrator." |
64	| "The missing move is the status check." | "The orchestrator checks the status." |
65	| "This keeps reviewable work from being stranded." | "This prevents stranded branches." |
66	
67	## The pruning safety rule
68	
69	Concision serves clarity, never the reverse. Before deleting a sentence, ask: does it carry a dependency, risk, assumption, or constraint? If yes, compress it into fewer words — do not remove it. A short document that omits the rollback plan is worse than a longer one that keeps it.
70	
```

```
1	# Engineering Design Docs / RFCs
2	
3	This file governs **how each section reads**, not which sections exist or their order. Inside the Shaper shape:design skill (design-doc track), that skill owns the section set, the gates, and the NFR taxonomy (`references/nfr-categories.md`); this file only refines the prose within whatever structure it mandates. Used standalone, treat the sections below as the conventional shape, not a gate.
4	
5	Map the five-phase workflow onto the design-doc sections. The reader should experience a story — where we are, what broke, what we will do — never a framework.
6	
7	## Section-by-section guidance
8	
9	**## Problem**
10	Open with the stable, accepted fact (the situation), then the disturbance and its cost. The undesired current state must be concrete: a number, an incident, a failing metric. End the section with the one-sentence answer — the recommended move — so the reader holds the conclusion before the detail.
11	
12	> Bad: "Latency has been a concern for some time and various stakeholders have expressed interest in improvements."
13	> Good: "Checkout completes in 1.2s today. Since the loyalty service launched in March, P95 has climbed to 4.1s, and conversion is down 6%. We will move loyalty lookups to an async path, restoring sub-1.5s checkout."
14	
15	**## Context**
16	Anchor the reader in stable, known facts before introducing anything new. Old before new applies at the section level: architecture the reader knows first, the recent change last.
17	
18	**## Constraints / NFRs**
19	Order by degree (most binding first) or by structure (latency / cost / compliance). Each constraint gets a number where one exists.
20	
21	**## Alternatives**
22	The set of alternatives must be MECE — no two options that partially overlap, no obvious option missing. Open each alternative with its summary effect ("Buys 5x headroom at 2x infra cost"), then the detail. State why each loses to the recommendation.
23	
24	**## Recommended Approach**
25	Old before new: start from the system as it is, walk to the proposed change. The newest component lands at the ends of sentences, where the stress position emphasizes it. Headings within this section are achievements ("Route loyalty calls through the event bus"), not labels ("Architecture").
26	
27	**## Operability / Rollout Plan**
28	Every gate gets a verifiable "Done when": "Done when canary error rate holds under 0.1% for 24h", not "monitor and proceed if healthy". Rollback criteria are operational facts — never prune them.
29	
30	## Design-doc review additions
31	
32	Beyond the universal Phase 5 gate, check:
33	
34	- Does the Problem section state the cost of the status quo in numbers?
35	- Is the recommendation stated in one sentence before the detail begins?
36	- Are alternatives genuinely exclusive and exhaustive?
37	- Do rollout gates have visualizable, verifiable end states?
38	- Did assumptions, risks, and dependencies survive pruning?
39	
```

605	      <div class="act-kicker">Scale</div>
606	      <h2 class="act-title">How far it runs</h2>
607	      <p class="act-tldr">The supervisor reaches further as observability earns trust: from one-shot CLI to resident daemon, from one issue to a whole planned unit, with review rolling up the same tree the work was broken down along.</p>
608	    </div>
